Archon Shiva — 3/15/25, 2:49 PM
Quick math: If loot is half coin and half gear, you'd convert 200 loot to 140 at normal wealth; that's 28 apiece.  Bring in the merc, converts to 200, takes 64 (30+34), you guys pocket 27.2 each.  You'd need to really optimize that ratio (such as accumulating sellable loot) to make it helpful.

Archon Shiva — 5/12/25, 1:03 PM
🌟 Protip 🌟  There are no prerequisites to Alchemy, and success at *crafting* potions barely matters.  Everyone can drop [1] into Alchemy-7 and make a potion of their choice each week - failure only means your weekly (e.g.) Minor Healing Potion costs $132 instead of $96.

Potions don't "cost" loot until they're used, and having an Alkahest or Magebane on hand at a critical moment can be *huge*.

Greybrown — 6/13/25, 12:22 AM
Since Michael/Adam is away this week, Vallium is sporting the Belt of Strength+1.  As Vael/Kyle is also away who is wearing the Ring of Free Action/who was wearing it last week?

Archon Shiva — 6/13/25, 1:11 AM
How does Free Action compare with the Swim spell?

dripton — 6/13/25, 1:37 AM
They both let you move at full move in the water.  They both let you defend and make melee attacks without water penalties.  Swim also lets you make ranged attacks without water penalties (but it won't make a weapon that absolutely does not work underwater work underwater).  Swim also lets you automatically succeed at Swimming rolls.

Archon Shiva — 6/13/25, 10:48 AM
Free Action allows vertical underwater movement?

Can you define "weapon that absolutely does not work underwater"?  Is that a flamethrower, a throwing axe, or a bow?

dripton — 6/13/25, 12:45 PM
Free Action lets you move at full speed underwater but does not automatically let you make swimming rolls, so you might go under.  Non-sealed guns, bows, and crossbows do not work at all underwater.  Throwing axes are not very effective underwater, unless you have a Swim spell, then they magically are.

Things that can kick your ass *and* outrun you are TPKs.

Greybrown — 6/14/25, 9:24 PM
Vallium has saved up 15+ character points.  My intent was and is to prioritize Combat Reflexes over Luck, but I am interested in hearing from the hive mind before I commit.

Archon Shiva — 6/14/25, 9:29 PM
CR is “Win More”: the times where it will help, you’re already doing okay.

Luck is for when your foe crits an eye shot and no amount of defense will help.

It’s also good for recovering from surprise, but if it comes to that, you could use Luck to reroll it.

Archon Shiva — 6/14/25, 9:32 PM
CR will let you parry one more attack this turn, but Luck will save you from landing on your back, dropping your weapon, falling unconscious, all of which are much worse than a failed parry now and then.

Demented Avenger — 6/14/25, 9:33 PM
Luck is the quintessential “live longer” advantage. Ioannes started this campaign without it and I felt nervous until I bought it

Demented Avenger — 6/14/25, 9:34 PM
Not only would I put Luck above Combat Reflexes, but I’d put Weapon Master above it, too

Archon Shiva — 6/14/25, 9:35 PM

Zuljita — 8/1/25, 12:45 AM
<@396077326129037345> do you have strong feelings one way or the other?

dripton — 8/1/25, 12:47 AM
As noted in the spell description, Pathfinder is really good for finding the best path to a place you've been before.  You get penalties for known places you haven't visited.  You get bigger penalties for places you've only heard rumors about.  I'm not saying it *can't* find the Fountain of Youth or the Seven Cities of Gold based on sketchy rumors, but the odds are not good.

dripton — 8/1/25, 12:48 AM
You're free to use it in play, or once per downtime.

Archon Shiva — 8/1/25, 1:02 AM
Or, or both?  Also, is One try per day for the spell as a whole, or by target?  (I think RAW by target, but spell as a whole may be better for the game)

dripton — 8/1/25, 1:08 AM
Once per downtime total; the usual minimizing downtime rolls.  During the game session each caster with the spell can use it once a day.  It's not totally clear if the "one try per day" in the RAW means per target or global.  Since a cheesy caster could easily work around "one try per day per location" by trying a bunch of locations known to be close together and triangulating/filtering outliers, I'm gonna say it's globally one try per day.

Archon Shiva — 8/1/25, 2:22 AM
I meant one in downtime and one in play.

Proposal for Session 23:

Rescue Lillian, priestess of Mitra.

Climb the Long Stair, make our way to the surface ruins entrance that lies northwest of the pyramid to make our way into the basement.  Go north to reach the stairwell that leads down a level to the Halls of Thoth.  Skirt west around the halfling territory and head south to the Children of Deino territory.  Announce ourselves and present a sizeable amount of food for trade. Be direct and truthful that we are seeking a certain person, Lillian, and that we would like to trade for her if she is in their custody, and if she is not, then we may wish to be escorted through their territory as Vael uses the Trace spell to locate her.  This is all built upon the assumption that the rumour that Lillian had been captured by "walking pigs" was referring to the Children of Deino, which may not be accurate.

Alternatively, if Vael has the Trace spell active before we reach Arden Vul (free to maintain), then we might alter course if it is clear that Lillian is not on the same level as the Children of Deino.

If we run into enough obstacles that prevent us from proceeding and exploring new territory (lose the Trace spell, Children of Deino swear they know nothing about her, etc.) the backup plan will be to return to the Well of Light, (buffing ourselves with the See Secrets spell if possible) and root out all the previously unexplored areas of that level.  Reaching the Well of Light from the Halls of Thoth can be done through using the teleporting pyramid in the southwest corner (assuming it always teleports to the same location).

Preparation: Large amounts of food for trade should be purchased.  This would be a good week to bring the mule along.  Vael should reserve the use of the Trace spell for casting it for Lillian.

Let me know if there are objections, suggestions or improvements.

Archon Shiva — 8/16/25, 3:18 PM
I guess I missed a conversation, but I generally think it’s best to get everyone on the same wavelength.  If the GM doesn’t want magic or other abilities to pass through walls, that’s a perfectly fine thing to house rule — but “I will make the tool available and expect you not to use it” makes analysis difficult from a PC point of view.

A GM saying “Here’s what I think should work and shouldn’t, let’s craft a house rule together” is great.

But also players can say enjoy “cheating” puzzles, or can prefer facing everything as intended.

It’s an important conversation.

Zuljita — 8/16/25, 4:01 PM

Proposal for Session 26:

Climb the Long Stair.  Enter through the basement ruins northwest of the pyramid.  Go east, but stop before entering the chamber that contains the bridge that spans the Great Chasm.

(Possibly scout at this point, using an Invisible Wizard's Eye.)

Give Flight (either Vael's spell or a Flight Potion, preferably through spellcasting due to the cost and scarcity of the Flight Potion) on both Michael and Vallium.  Give Invisibility to Michael and Vallium (either Vael's spell, and Invisibility Potion or the friendship rings.  Having one Invisibility being used through the ring might be good, as the cessation of blindness will indicate that the fight has started to those not in the fray). Their Light stones should be put away.

Invisible Michael and Vallium will enter Plumthorn's room.  Vallium will use a Magebane Grenade on the wizard, while Michael attempts to cut down Phlebotomas. Vallium will Quick-Draw his sword to cut down the wizard and join Michael in taking out what is hopefully Phlembotomas, the wizard and the two halfling guards stationed in the room.

If this fracas somehow doesn't attract the attention of other halflings perhaps we can attempt some shenanigans (Ring of Illusion Disguise to emulate the wizard, etc.), but we should expect incoming enemies.  Vallium and Michael should attempt to bar the door (we don't want only two people involved in the fight, from a meta-perspective, this is boring for the rest of the party).  Perhaps one flight-endowed fighter can accompany the rest of the party via carrying/Walk on Air/Levitation to Phlebotomas' chamber across the Chasm.

Greybrown — 9/28/25, 5:10 PM
At this point, the party moves west and upon encountering halflings attempts to provide proof of Phlebotomas' demise, and demand the halfling's surrender and exile. (Fun idea to try to take them over, but we did promise the Goblin King their territory).  Vael should make use of Larel's Cloak and the Amulet of the Silver Tongue for the bonuses they provide to this negotiation.  I have no issue with them taking mundane gear, but we should be confiscating magic items, wands, potions, money, etc if they are amenable to leaving.  Potentially we might have to use deadly force if they refuse to yield.

If we managed to clear the halfling territory and search it thoroughly for valuables, we should make our way to the goblin territory to inform them that we're gifting them with the area.  Collect our reward for returning the king's brother, and potentially a reward for seizing the halfling territory.  If there is time remaining we can ask for a tour if the lower level of goblin territory, as we would otherwise gain no XP for exploration for this session.

Should we bring additional gear, by which I mean the statue head trap with amber eyes, or the red or yellow glass spheres?  With the Bags of Holding, the 50 lb head becomes a possibility.  

Lot of possibilities here, and I don't think we'll be able to accurately predict what will happen after the initial strike on Phlebotomas.

Asking Questions: Who knows what?

**Anaximander:** - Events from ~250? years ago, Adrienic.  Possibly knows more about Lankios, K?, and Guivrel?

**Lankios:** - Events from ~250? years ago, Adrienic.  Maybe not fully sane?

**Kel Bormol:** - ~1200? years ago, era of civial war. Possibly very dangerous, probably not worth the risk. Overseer.

**Bronze Heads:** Untested. Need to find one to use in private. Hopefully steal one to keep on the Beacon for ourselves. "Turns off", unknown how long they operate for.

**Spirit of Jaken the Proud:** Unknown, but appears to be a contemporary of Arden and Vul. ~2150 years ago? Can only be summoned once a month apparently.

**Avatar of Bastet:** Theological matters? Can only contact once a month, on the full moon, for 3 questions. Specifically related to Cult of Bastet.  As the Cult resides deep within Arden Vul, some details of Arden Vul might be known.  Ask best route to get to Cult of Bastet? Ask what to say to a Sister People of Bastet so that they will know we are friends?

**Akla-Chah:** Rudishva matters. How long ago did the Beacon crash? Over 3 millenia ago, certainly pre-Archontean discovery of Arden Vul?  Knowledge of surviving rudishva could be useful.  Also, useful tool for compiling knowledge.

**Stamelis:** Librarian and priest of Thoth, active   ~1.3k-1.2k ago, including civil war. Withholds knowledge of Thothian priesthood.

**Ptarmis:** 2000 years ago? Early days of Arden Vul.  Probably knowledge of the Great Cavern, Herb Lore, other druid stuff.  Might be insane from age and isolation, or might not be original Ptarmis as tomb of Ptarmis is right there also.

**Obelisk of Painful Knowledge:** Ask Thoth a question, receive 3d6 damage and an answer.  Unknown if there are limits to frequency. Thoth related questions are probably best, and there are certainly a number of threads to pull given Arden Vul's upper levels.

**Ptirasa:** Priestess of Thoth ghost.  Seems nice.

Archon Shiva — 3/1/26, 8:49 PM
<@439963160502337536> <@630407861536686109> I'd like to draw your attention to Awaken as a cheaper (and preemptive) alternative to Lend Energy in the middle of combat: "A subject with less than 1/3 [but >0] of basic FP remaining may ignore the ill effects of fatigue for an hour but loses 1 FP at the end of that time"

It's an area spell, so it's either 2-3FP to "fix" multiple PCs, or 0FP for a single target (Ioannes, with discount).

Much better on Basilisk than on Vael, but works kinda well for everyone; it's worth 2-4 FP per PC, depending who it's cast on.

Demented Avenger — 4/3/26, 9:18 PM
How's this? I keep the whole game in some ChatGPT threads:

How to Run the Arena Mission Properly

Phase 1 — Forum check-in
    •    Drop off food
    •    Talk to Gribble
    •    Ask what’s changed

Phase 2 — Route finding
    •    Try to connect Goblin Market → Arena
    •    Move cautiously but not fearfully
    •    Mark path mentally or physically

Phase 3 — Arena behavior (important)

You are not there to fight.

You are there to:
    •    Observe who’s present
    •    Identify factions
    •    Ask about:
    •    Command Bracelet rumors
    •    Recent disturbances
    •    “interesting areas nearby”

Think like a spy, not a delver.

⸻

Phase 4 — Opportunistic XP

You might get:
    •    Exploration credit (likely)
    •    Maybe a small treasure opportunity (don’t force it)

⸻

Phase 5 — Leave early

Don’t linger once you’ve learned something.

⸻

The One Thing to Bring

Vallium is absolutely right about this:

Bring a See Secrets item

That’s your edge tonight.

Because you’re not fighting—you’re spotting things others miss.

⸻

What You Should NOT Do in the Arena
    •    Don’t pick fights
    •    Don’t investigate suspicious doors deeply
    •    Don’t follow anyone into back areas
    •    Don’t “just check one corridor”

You’re there to learn, not clear.

Belts aren't armor.  Boots are armor.

Archon Shiva — 5/2/26, 4:49 AM
It's pretty straightforward: The feature is a game mechanic affecting armor, which appears on pages 108-109, with boots defined as foot armor, whereas a belt is a combat accessory and defined as clothing on page 111.  The box on page 110 specifically explains how clothing is not armor (and therefore you can wear a shirt in town without a Reaction penalty, but not armor.
